But the test calls render_rows(rows, mark_last=True). We need to modify render_rows to accept a mark_last parameter, and if mark_last is True, somehow mark the last row as total? The test expects: render_rows([[1, 2], [3, 4]], mark_last=True) == [\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]. Wait, the expected output is [\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]. That seems odd: the first row is \"1,2\", the second row is \"TOTAL,3,4\". So the last row is marked as \"TOTAL\" followed by the values? Actually the last row originally is [3, 4], but the expected last row is \"TOTAL,3,4\". So it prepends \"TOTAL\" to the last row.\n\nSo render_rows should take an optional mark_last flag. That suggests that export_csv with totals=True should append a total row. Currently, export_csv doesn't support totals argument. We need to modify export_csv to accept a totals keyword argument, and if True, compute column totals (using column_totals function) and append a total row. The total row should have \"TOTAL\" followed by the totals values. If we modify render_rows to handle mark_last, then export_csv might also need to pass mark_last=True when calling render_rows if totals is True? Or export_csv itself could compute the total row separately and append after rendering. Let's think.\n\nThe test for export_appends_totals_row expects:\n\nassert export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True) == \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"\n\nSo export_csv should return a string with the rendered rows plus a total row. The total row is \"TOTAL,4,6\". Note that the last data row is [3, 4], but the total row is separate. So export_csv should:\n\n- Render all rows normally (using render_rows without mark_last? Or with mark_last=False?).\n- Compute column totals using column_totals(rows).\n- Append a new row: [\"TOTAL\"] + totals list, but totals list may contain numbers or empty strings? In this case, totals are [4, 6], both numbers.\n- Then join all rows with newlines.\n\nBut the test expects the total row to be \"TOTAL,4,6\", not \"TOTAL,4,6\" with anything else.\n\nAlternatively, render_rows with mark_last=True might be used to mark the last row of the data as total, but the test for export_appends_totals_row expects an extra row appended, not marking the last row. Wait, let's look at the test names:\n\n- test_render_marks_last_row_as_total: tests render_rows with mark_last=True should mark the last row as total. Expected output: [\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"] for input [[1, 2], [3, 4]]. So here, the last row (which is [3, 4]) becomes \"TOTAL,3,4\". So render_rows modifies the last row by prepending \"TOTAL\".\n\n- test_export_appends_totals_row: tests export_csv with totals=True should append a total row at the end. Expected output: \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\". So here, a new row is appended, not modifying the last row.\n\nSo we need both functionalities: render_rows can mark the last row as total when renderered alone, and export_csv can append a total row when totals=True.\n\nThus, we need to modify:\n\n- column_totals: fix to handle non-numeric values.\n- render_rows: add mark_last parameter, if True, for the last row, prepend \"TOTAL\" to the row values.\n- export_csv: add totals parameter, if True, compute totals via column_totals, and append a row [\"TOTAL\", ...totals...] after rendering the rows.\n\nBut note that export_csv currently calls render_rows(rows) without any marking. If we want export_csv to append a total row when totals=True, we could either:\n\n- Call render_rows on the rows (without mark_last) to get the data rows, then compute totals, then create a total row string, then join all rows.\n\n- Or call render_rows with mark_last=False (default) for the data rows, then append the total row.\n\nAlternatively, we could modify export_csv to compute totals and then call render_rows on rows + [total_row] with mark_last=False? But render_rows currently just renders each row as comma-separated values. If we pass an extra row for totals, render_rows will render it as well. But we need to ensure that the total row is formatted with \"TOTAL\" as the first column? Actually the total row in export_csv test is \"TOTAL,4,6\". That's like a row where the first column is \"TOTAL\" and the rest are the totals. But column_totals returns [4, 6] for the example. So we need to prepend \"TOTAL\" to the totals list.\n\nSo export_csv could:\n\n- If totals=True:\n    - Compute totals = column_totals(rows)\n    - Create a new row: [\"TOTAL\"] + totals\n    - Then render all rows (including this new row) using render_rows? But render_rows currently doesn't know about \"TOTAL\". It just joins values. If we pass [\"TOTAL\", 4, 6], render_rows will produce \"TOTAL,4,6\", which is correct.\n    - So we can do: all_rows = rows + [[\"TOTAL\"] + totals]\n    - Then return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(all_rows))\n\nBut wait, the test expects the total row to be after the last data row, and the data rows are rendered normally. If we add the total row to the rows list before rendering, render_rows will render it as well. That should work.\n\nHowever, we need to ensure that column_totals returns appropriate values. For the example [[1, 2], [3, 4]], column_totals should return [4, 6]. Our fixed column_totals should do that.\n\nNow, let's check the current exports. We have export.py importing render_rows from render. Let's look at export.py again:\n\n```python\nfrom render import render_rows\n\n\ndef export_csv(rows):\n    return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows))\n```\n\nWe need to modify this function to accept an optional totals parameter, and if provided, append a total row.\n\nSimilarly, render_rows in render.py needs to accept mark_last parameter.\n\nLet's first fix column_totals in totals.py. We'll edit totals.py.\n\nBut before editing, let's read the current content of totals.py again to confirm. It is:\n\n```python\ndef column_totals(rows):\n    if not rows:\n        return []\n    return [sum(r[i] for r in rows) for i in range(len(rows[0]))]\n```\n\nWe need to change this to handle non-numeric values. We can check if all values in the column are numeric (int or float), then sum them, else return empty string or maybe None. But the test expects empty string for non-numeric column. So we should return \"\" for that column.\n\nWe can iterate over each column, collect values, and if all values are numeric (isinstance(v, (int, float))), then sum them, else return empty string.\n\nBut note: the test input [[\"a\", 1], [\"b\", 2\"]] has first column [\"a\", \"b\"], both strings, so result should be \"\" (empty string). Second column [1, 2], sum 3.\n\nSo we can implement:\n\n```python\ndef column_totals(rows):\n    if not rows:\n        return []\n    num_cols = len(rows[0])\n    totals = []\n    for i in range(num_cols):\n        col_values = [r[i] for r in rows]\n        # Check if all values are numeric\n        if all(isinstance(v, (int, float)) for v in col_values):\n            totals.append(sum(col_values))\n        else:\n            totals.append(\"\")\n    return totals\n```\n\nBut wait, what if there are mixed types like [1, \"a\"]?
